So, I thought I should probably come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things?\n\nPatient: Yes... I suppose I do. My mind just keeps... circling around things. Mostly regarding the practice. I'm in the process of handing it over, so... I keep thinking about whether my old patients are being looked after properly. Or if the handoff is actually financially sound. \n\nAnd... well, just the thought of being fully retired. Whether I'll just become... useless, I suppose. I know the amount I worry about it all feels a bit out of proportion, even to me, but... yes. That's where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden episode of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. It doesn't really happen suddenly like that. So, no. I haven't had anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain things or situations because of your worries?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I mean, I still go into the clinic, I still paint. I just... worry while I'm doing them, I suppose. But I don't avoid things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I'm not... sad. Just... preoccupied, I suppose. But not down.\n\nDoctor: Has something traumatic or very stressful happened to you recently?\n\nPatient: No... nothing like that. Just the usual day-to-day, really. Nothing out of the ordinary.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to stop thinking about them?\n\nPatient: Yes... I suppose they do. I try to reason with myself, really. Sort of the way I would with a nervous patient in the chair. But... it doesn't quite work. One concern just leads to the next. And I just end up... checking my emails again, or reviewing my clinic notes... asking the same questions instead of just letting it go.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical symptoms like trembling, sweating, or a racing heart when you're worried?\n\nPatient: No... I haven't noticed anything like that. My hands are quite steady. They have to be, for the practice. I don't really get... sweaty, or anything.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I'll be at the clinic reviewing a patient's chart, or even just at home trying to lay out a watercolor wash, and... I'll realize I've read the same line three times. My mind just sort of wanders off to... well, to everything that could go wrong. So, I thought I should probably come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things?\n\nPatient: Yes... I suppose I do. My mind just keeps... circling around things. Mostly regarding the practice. I'm in the process of handing it over, so... I keep thinking about whether my old patients are being looked after properly. Or if the handoff is actually financially sound. \n\nAnd... well, just the thought of being fully retired. Whether I'll just become... useless, I suppose. I know the amount I worry about it all feels a bit out of proportion, even to me, but... yes. That's where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden episode of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. It doesn't really happen suddenly like that. So, no. I haven't had anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain things or situations because of your worries?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I mean, I still go into the clinic, I still paint. I just... worry while I'm doing them, I suppose. But I don't avoid things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I'm not... sad. Just... preoccupied, I suppose. But not down.\n\nDoctor: Has something traumatic or very stressful happened to you recently?\n\nPatient: No... nothing like that. Just the usual day-to-day, really. Nothing out of the ordinary.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to stop thinking about them?\n\nPatient: Yes... I suppose they do. I try to reason with myself, really. Sort of the way I would with a nervous patient in the chair. But... it doesn't quite work. One concern just leads to the next. And I just end up... checking my emails again, or reviewing my clinic notes... asking the same questions instead of just letting it go.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical symptoms like trembling, sweating, or a racing heart when you're worried?\n\nPatient: No... I haven't noticed anything like that. My hands are quite steady. They have to be, for the practice. I don't really get... sweaty, or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control these repeating thoughts?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose so. Like I said, I... I do try to talk myself down. You know, be rational about it. But it just... it doesn't seem to stop them. I just keep going over the notes, checking the emails... I can't seem to just put it down.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these worries and difficulties with concentration?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it's been a while now. It started... oh, maybe a year and three months ago. Right around the time I began the formal process to retire.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform specific rituals or behaviors to reduce your worries?\n\nPatient: No... nothing like that. I don't have any rituals. Just... what I already mentioned, about checking the clinic notes and the emails. That's really it.\n\nDoctor: Are all your worries centered around your retirement and the clinic, or do you find yourself worrying about other things as well?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it's a mix of things. My mind just keeps circling. \n\nI do think about the clinic a lot, yes... whether my old patients are being looked after properly by the new team. And the handoff, just making sure it's all financially sound. But then it's also... well, other things. Like whether I'll just become useless once I'm fully retired. \n\nI know it's... I know the amount I worry about all this is out of proportion. I just can't seem to shut it off.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that are intrusive or unwanted, and that you try to ignore or suppress?\n\nPatient: No... I wouldn't say that. I mean, obviously I'd rather not be worrying so much, but... they're just my regular thoughts. I'm not trying to suppress anything. Right around the time I began the formal process to retire.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform specific rituals or behaviors to reduce your worries?\n\nPatient: No... nothing like that. I don't have any rituals. Just... what I already mentioned, about checking the clinic notes and the emails. I suppose it's a mix of things. My mind just keeps circling. \n\nI do think about the clinic a lot, yes... whether my old patients are being looked after properly by the new team. I know the amount I worry about all this is out of proportion. I just can't seem to shut it off.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that are intrusive or unwanted, and that you try to ignore or suppress?\n\nPatient: No... I wouldn't say that. My mind just sort of wanders off to... well, to everything that could go wrong. So, I thought I should probably come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things?\n\nPatient: Yes... I suppose I do. My mind just keeps... circling around things. Mostly regarding the practice. I'm in the process of handing it over, so... I keep thinking about whether my old patients are being looked after properly. Or if the handoff is actually financially sound. \n\nAnd... well, just the thought of being fully retired. Whether I'll just become... useless, I suppose. I know the amount I worry about it all feels a bit out of proportion, even to me, but... yes. That's where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden episode of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. It doesn't really happen suddenly like that. So, no. I haven't had anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain things or situations because of your worries?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I mean, I still go into the clinic, I still paint. I just... worry while I'm doing them, I suppose. But I don't avoid things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I'm not... sad. Just... preoccupied, I suppose. But not down.\n\nDoctor: Has something traumatic or very stressful happened to you recently?\n\nPatient: No... nothing like that. Just the usual day-to-day, really. Nothing out of the ordinary.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to stop thinking about them?\n\nPatient: Yes... I suppose they do. I try to reason with myself, really. Sort of the way I would with a nervous patient in the chair. But... it doesn't quite work. One concern just leads to the next. And I just end up... checking my emails again, or reviewing my clinic notes... asking the same questions instead of just letting it go.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical symptoms like trembling, sweating, or a racing heart when you're worried?\n\nPatient: No... I haven't noticed anything like that. My hands are quite steady. They have to be, for the practice. I don't really get... sweaty, or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control these repeating thoughts?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose so. Like I said, I... I do try to talk myself down. You know, be rational about it. But it just... it doesn't seem to stop them. I just keep going over the notes, checking the emails... I can't seem to just put it down.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these worries and difficulties with concentration?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it's been a while now. It started... oh, maybe a year and three months ago. Right around the time I began the formal process to retire.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform specific rituals or behaviors to reduce your worries?\n\nPatient: No... nothing like that. I don't have any rituals. Just... what I already mentioned, about checking the clinic notes and the emails. That's really it.\n\nDoctor: Are all your worries centered around your retirement and the clinic, or do you find yourself worrying about other things as well?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it's a mix of things. My mind just keeps circling. \n\nI do think about the clinic a lot, yes... whether my old patients are being looked after properly by the new team. And the handoff, just making sure it's all financially sound. But then it's also... well, other things. Like whether I'll just become useless once I'm fully retired. \n\nI know it's... I know the amount I worry about all this is out of proportion. I just can't seem to shut it off.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that are intrusive or unwanted, and that you try to ignore or suppress?\n\nPatient: No... I wouldn't say that. I mean, obviously I'd rather not be worrying so much, but... they're just my regular thoughts. I'm not trying to suppress anything. It's just... what's on my mind.\n\nDoctor: Are your worries affecting your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: Not really... I mostly keep it to myself, so... things are fine at home. I don't think it's getting in the way of anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Are your worries interfering with your ability to enjoy your watercolor painting or other activities you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose so. The painting, and... gardening, too. They just don't feel as manageable as they used to. \n\nAnd... I guess it's not just that. I've actually had to slow down and postpone some parts of the retirement handoff. I just... I keep second-guessing my decisions, and I end up losing time at the clinic. So... yes, it's making things a bit difficult.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worries are causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Distress? ... I don't know if I'd use that word. It's... it's frustrating, certainly. But I wouldn't say I'm in distress. It's just... a bit much sometimes, I suppose. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these worries are occupying your mind in a way that's excessive and not really related to any real problem?\n\nPatient: I suppose... my mind does just keep circling around things. They are real things, mostly, but... maybe I dwell on them too much. \n\nJust things like... whether my old patients are being looked after properly by the new team. Or if the practice handoff is actually financially sound. And... well, what happens when I'm fully retired. If I'll just end up being... useless, I suppose. \n\nSo... yes, I guess the amount of worry feels a bit out of proportion. Even to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. But I don't avoid things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I'm not... sad. Just... preoccupied, I suppose. But not down.\n\nDoctor: Has something traumatic or very stressful happened to you recently?\n\nPatient: No... nothing like that. Just the usual day-to-day, really. Nothing out of the ordinary.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to stop thinking about them?\n\nPatient: Yes... I suppose they do. I try to reason with myself, really. Sort of the way I would with a nervous patient in the chair. But... it doesn't quite work. One concern just leads to the next. And I just end up... checking my emails again, or reviewing my clinic notes... asking the same questions instead of just letting it go.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical symptoms like trembling, sweating, or a racing heart when you're worried?\n\nPatient: No... I haven't noticed anything like that. My hands are quite steady. They have to be, for the practice. I don't really get... sweaty, or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control these repeating thoughts?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose so. Like I said, I... I do try to talk myself down. You know, be rational about it. But it just... it doesn't seem to stop them. I just keep going over the notes, checking the emails... I can't seem to just put it down.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these worries and difficulties with concentration?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it's been a while now. It started... oh, maybe a year and three months ago. Right around the time I began the formal process to retire.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform specific rituals or behaviors to reduce your worries?\n\nPatient: No... nothing like that. I don't have any rituals. Just... what I already mentioned, about checking the clinic notes and the emails. That's really it.\n\nDoctor: Are all your worries centered around your retirement and the clinic, or do you find yourself worrying about other things as well?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it's a mix of things. My mind just keeps circling. \n\nI do think about the clinic a lot, yes... whether my old patients are being looked after properly by the new team. And the handoff, just making sure it's all financially sound. But then it's also... well, other things. Like whether I'll just become useless once I'm fully retired. \n\nI know it's... I know the amount I worry about all this is out of proportion. I just can't seem to shut it off.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that are intrusive or unwanted, and that you try to ignore or suppress?\n\nPatient: No... I wouldn't say that. I mean, obviously I'd rather not be worrying so much, but... they're just my regular thoughts. I'm not trying to suppress anything. It's just... what's on my mind.\n\nDoctor: Are your worries affecting your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: Not really... I mostly keep it to myself, so... things are fine at home. I don't think it's getting in the way of anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Are your worries interfering with your ability to enjoy your watercolor painting or other activities you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose so. The painting, and... gardening, too. They just don't feel as manageable as they used to. \n\nAnd... I guess it's not just that. I've actually had to slow down and postpone some parts of the retirement handoff. I just... I keep second-guessing my decisions, and I end up losing time at the clinic. So... yes, it's making things a bit difficult.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worries are causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Distress? ... I don't know if I'd use that word. It's... it's frustrating, certainly. But I wouldn't say I'm in distress. It's just... a bit much sometimes, I suppose. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these worries are occupying your mind in a way that's excessive and not really related to any real problem?\n\nPatient: I suppose... my mind does just keep circling around things. They are real things, mostly, but... maybe I dwell on them too much. \n\nJust things like... whether my old patients are being looked after properly by the new team. Or if the practice handoff is actually financially sound. And... well, what happens when I'm fully retired. If I'll just end up being... useless, I suppose. \n\nSo... yes, I guess the amount of worry feels a bit out of proportion. Even to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like any of these worries are completely unreasonable or nonsensical to you?\n\nPatient: Nonsensical? ... No. I wouldn't say that. \n\nThey're... they are real concerns. The clinic, the transition... it's not nonsense. I just... maybe I focus on them a bit too heavily. But no, I don't think they're completely unreasonable.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to take time off work or significantly reduce your work hours due to your worries?\n\nPatient: Time off? No, not exactly. But... I have had to slow down the retirement handoff. Postpone parts of it, mostly. \n\nI just... I keep second-guessing the decisions, and I end up losing time at the clinic because of it. And... I don't know, even at home, things like my painting or getting out in the garden... they just don't feel quite as manageable lately.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, obviously I'd rather not be worrying so much, but... they're just my regular thoughts. I'm not trying to suppress anything. It's just... what's on my mind.\n\nDoctor: Are your worries affecting your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: Not really... I mostly keep it to myself, so... things are fine at home. I don't think it's getting in the way of anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Are your worries interfering with your ability to enjoy your watercolor painting or other activities you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Well... I've actually had to slow down and postpone some parts of the retirement handoff. I just... I keep second-guessing my decisions, and I end up losing time at the clinic. So... yes, it's making things a bit difficult.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worries are causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Distress? ... I don't know if I'd use that word. It's... it's frustrating, certainly. But I wouldn't say I'm in distress. It's just... a bit much sometimes, I suppose. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these worries are occupying your mind in a way that's excessive and not really related to any real problem?\n\nPatient: I suppose... my mind does just keep circling around things. No. I wouldn't say that. \n\nThey're... they are real concerns. The clinic, the transition... it's not nonsense. I just... maybe I focus on them a bit too heavily.
